**וַיֵּצֵא הָרוּחַ.** פִּתְרוֹן: וַיֵּצֵא הַמַּלְאָךְ; שֶׁהֲרֵי אוֹמֵר לְעֵיל **וְכָל צְבָא הַשָּׁמַיִם וְגוֹ׳**, וְעַתָּה יָצָא אֶחָד מִן הַמַּלְאָכִים וַיֹּאמֶר **אֲנִי אֲפַתֶּנּוּ**; כְּדַאֲמַר ״עוֹשֶׂה מַלְאָכָיו רוּחוֹת״ (תהלים קד:ד). וּלְפִיכָךְ קָדַם וְאָמַר לְעֵיל **וְכָל צְבָא הַשָּׁמַיִם עוֹמְדִים**, שֶׁאַל תִּתְמַהּ כְּשֶׁתֹּאמַר וַיֵּצֵא הָרוּחַ, מִי הוּא וּמֵהֵיכָן יָצָא; וּכְשֶׁאָמַר **וְכָל צְבָא הַשָּׁמַיִם עוֹמְדִים**, כְּדֵי לְהוֹדִיעַ מֵהֵיכָן יָצָא מִשְּׁאָר הָרוּחוֹת הָעוֹמְדִים עָלָיו. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ (סנהדרין קב:): זֶהוּ רוּחוֹ שֶׁל נָבוֹת:


**And a certain spirit came forth** The meaning is: And an angel came forth, for he says above, “and all the host of heaven, etc.” One of the angels came forth and said, “I will entice him,” as it is stated (Ps. 104:4): “He makes His angels spirits.” [Cf. Comm. Dig. ad loc.] He preceded this account by stating, “and all the host of heaven were standing,” so that you should not wonder when you read, “And a certain spirit came forth,” who he was and where he came from. When he says, “and all the host of heaven were standing,” it is to inform you where he came from: from among the rest of the spirits standing beside Him. Our Rabbis (*Sanh.* 89a, *Targum*) however, explained that this was the spirit of Naboth.



### Verse 21

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:21:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:21:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:21:1)


**בְּפִי כָּל נְבִיאָיו.** שֶׁיָּשִׂים דָּבָר בְּפִיהֶם לֵאמֹר **עֲלֵה וְהַצְלַח** וְיַעֲלֶה **וְיִפֹּל**, מִכְּלָל שֶׁהָיוּ נְבִיאֵי אֱמֶת שֶׁלֹּא הָיוּ מִתְנַבְּאִים כִּי אִם מַה שֶׁיָּשִׂים הַשֵּׁם בְּפִיהֶם:


**in the mouth of all his prophets** He should place the words “Go up and triumph,” in their mouths and he [Ahab] will go up and fall. This proves that they were true prophets who would prophesy only that which the Lord put into their mouths.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:21:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:21:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:21:2)


**תְּפַתֶּה וְגַם תּוּכָל.** לְפִי שֶׁיֵּשׁ מְפַתֶּה הַבָּא לְפַתּוֹת חֲבֵירוֹ, פְּעָמִים שֶׁהַמְּפֻתֶּה מֵבִין שֶׁזֶּה רוֹצֶה לְפַתּוֹתוֹ וְהוּא נִזְהָר וְאֵינוֹ שׁוֹמֵעַ לְהִתְפַּתּוֹת, אֲבָל בְּאַחְאָב תְּפַתֶּה וְגַם תּוּכָל:


**You will entice and you will prevail** When an enticer comes to entice his fellow, the one being enticed sometimes understands that this one wishes to entice him, and he takes heed and does not allow himself to be enticed, but with Ahab, you will entice and you will prevail.



### Verse 24

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:24:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:24:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:24:1)


**הִנְּךָ רֹאֶה.** שֶׁיֵּהָרֵג אַחְאָב:


**Behold you will see** that Ahab will be killed.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:26:1)


**שִׂימוּ זֶה בֵּית הַכֶּלֶא.** וְאַף עַל פִּי שֶׁלֹּא רָאִינוּ שֶׁנְּתָנוּ קֹדֶם לָכֵן בְּבֵית הַכֶּלֶא, אֶלָּא מִתּוֹךְ שֶׁאָמַר **הֲשִׁיבֻהוּ**, תּוּכַל לוֹמַר שֶׁנְּתָנוּ קֹדֶם לָכֵן בְּבֵית הַכֶּלֶא. וְאִם תִּרְצֶה לוֹמַר שֶׁלֹּא נְתָנוּהוּ קֹדֶם לָכֵן מֵעוֹלָם, לֹא תִקְשֶׁה לְךָ **וַהֲשִׁיבֻהוּ**, כִּי כֵּן דֶּרֶךְ הַמִּקְרָאוֹת, כְּמוֹ ״יָשׁוּבוּ רְשָׁעִים לִשְׁאוֹלָה״ (תהלים ט:יח) וְכֵן הַרְבֵּה:


**Place this person in prison** We did not see that he incarcerated him in prison previously, but since he says, “and return him,” you may say that he had placed him in prison beforehand. If, however, you wish to say that they had never placed him in prison prior to this, “and return him” should not cause you any difficulty, because this is the style of the Scriptures, like (Ps. 9:18): “May the wicked return to the grave,” and many similar other cases.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:26:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:26:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:26:2)


**לֶחֶם לַחַץ וּמַיִם לַחַץ.** דֻּגְמָא: ״לֶחֶם צַר וּמַיִם לַחַץ״ (ישעיהו ל:כ):


**a scant amount of bread, etc.** An example of this is (Isa. 30: 20): “scant bread and water of oppression.”



### Verse 29

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:29:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:29:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:29:1)


**הִתְחַפֵּשׂ וָבוֹא.** פִּתְרוֹן: צָרִיךְ אֲנִי לְהִתְנַכֵּר וּלְהִתְחַפֵּשׂ בִּבְגָדִים אֲחֵרִים, שֶׁלֹּא יַכִּירוּנִי שֶׁאֲנִי מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל; כִּי חָשַׁשׁ לְדִבְרֵי מִיכָיְהוּ שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ: **אִם שׁוֹב תָּשׁוּב בְּשָׁלוֹם וְגוֹ׳**, וְאָמַר לִיהוֹשָׁפָט: אֲנִי רוֹצֶה לְהִשְׁתַּנּוֹת עַצְמִי וְלָבוֹא **בַּמִּלְחָמָה**, שֶׁיֵּשׁ לִי לָחוּשׁ שֶׁמָּא שָׁמְעוּ אֲרַמִּים מַה שֶּׁאָמַר מִיכָיְהוּ **אִם שׁוֹב תָּשׁוּב בְּשָׁלוֹם**, וְלֹא יָסֹבּוּ לְהִלָּחֵם כִּי אִם עָלַי:


**disguise myself and go** The meaning is: “I must make myself like a stranger and disguise myself in other clothes, so that they do not recognize that I am the king of Israel,” for he was wary of Micaiah’s words, saying, “If you return in peace, etc.” And he said to Jehoshaphat, “I wish to disguise myself and go to war, for I fear that the Arameans heard that Micaiah said, ‘If you return in peace,’ and they will attack only me.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:29:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:29:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:29:2)


**וְאַתָּה לְבַשׁ בְּגָדֶיךָ.** תַּכְסִיסֵי מַלְכוּת וְשִׁרְיוֹנוֹת וּכְלֵי מִלְחָמָה שֶׁל מַלְכוּת, כִּי אֵין לָחוּשׁ בְּדִבְרֵי הַנָּבִיא כִּי אִם מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל לְבַדּוֹ, כִּי כֵן הִגִּידוּ מְרַגְּלִים שֶׁלּוֹ דִּבְרֵי מִיכָיְהוּ:


**but you may wear your garb** The royal costume and the coat of mail and the royal weapons, for there is no need for anyone to be wary of the prophet’s words except the king of Israel, for his [the king of Aram’s] spies told [him] the words of Micaiah.



### Verse 31

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:31:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:31:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:31:1)


**וַיִּזְעַק יְהוֹשָׁפָט.** אַחַר בְּנֵי דִגְלוֹ וְחַיָּלוֹתָיו שֶׁיָּבֹאוּ לַעֲזֹר לוֹ, כְּמוֹ אוֹת אַרְיֵה שֶׁבְּדִגְלוֹ אוֹ דֶּגֶל בְּנֵי יְהוּדָה. וְכֵן מִנְהָג כְּשֶׁנִּלְחָמִים שְׁנֵי גְדוּדִים, מְכַנֶּה כָּל גְּדוּד וּגְדוּד שֵׁם אֶחָד לְסִימָן לְדִגְלוֹ לְצֹרֶךְ הַגְּדוּד שֶׁעִמּוֹ, שֶׁאִם יִתְפָּרְדוּ אִישׁ מֵעַל אָחִיו אוֹ יִתְעָרְבוּ הַגְּדוּדִים זֶה בָּזֶה, יִצְעֲקוּ כָּל גְּדוּד וּגְדוּד שֵׁם סִימָן שֶׁלּוֹ וְיַכִּירוּ בְּקוֹל הַסִּימָן וְיִתְקַבְּצוּ כֻּלָּם יַחַד וְיִהְיוּ בַּאֲגֻדָּה אַחַת כְּבָרִאשׁוֹנָה; וְלֹא שֶׁצָּעַק לֵאלֹהָיו. וְכֵן מוֹכִיחַ: ״וַיִּזְעַק יְהוֹשָׁפָט״ (מלכים א כב:לב), וְלֹא נֶאֱמַר לַשֵּׁם **וַה׳ עֲזָרוֹ וַיְסִיתֵם אֱלֹהִים מִמֶּנּוּ**, אַךְ כְּתִיב שָׁם ״וַיְהִי כִּרְאוֹת שָׂרֵי הָרֶכֶב כִּי לֹא״ הָיָה ״מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל״ (מלכים א כב:לג), שֶׁהִכִּירוּ וְהֵבִינוּ מִיָּד כְּשֶׁצָּעַק אַחַר הַסִּימָן שֶׁל דִּגְלוֹ, כִּדְמְפָרֵשׁ כְּמוֹ שֶׁהִכִּירוּהוּ, ״וַיָּשׁוּבוּ״ מֵעָלָיו (מלכים א כב:לג) כְּצִוּוּי מֶלֶךְ אֲרָם; וּמֵה׳ הוּא שֶׁהִכִּירוּהוּ:


**and Jehoshaphat cried out** He called to his troop and his armies to come to his aid, [using] the sign of the lion on his banner or the banner of the sons of Judah. And so is the custom when two troops engage in battle: each troop gives a name to the insignia of its division for the troop that is with it, so that if they separate from each other or if the troops mingle with one another, each troop shouts the name of its insignia, and they recognize the insignia and all gather together and become one group as originally. He did not, however, cry out to his God, as is proven from I Kings (22:32), for there it does not say, “and the Lord helped him, and God enticed them away from him,” but it is written, **And it was when the officers of the chariots saw that he was not the king of Israel** for they immediately recognized and understood when he cried out the insignia of his division, as was explained. As soon as they recognized him, they turned away from him in accordance with the order of the king of Aram, and it was from the Lord that they recognized him.



### Verse 33

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:33:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:1)


**וְאִישׁ מָשַׁךְ בַּקֶּשֶׁת.** אִישׁ יִשְׂרָאֵל מִתְכַּוֵּין לִירוֹת בְּמַחֲנֵה אֲרָם, וּבְלֹא כַוָּנָתוֹ:


**And a man drew his bow** An Israelite man intended to shoot at the camp of Aram, and inadvertently


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:33:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:2)


**וַיַּךְ.** הַחֵץ אֶת מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**and it hit** The arrow [hit] the king of Israel.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:33:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:3)


**בֵּין הַדְּבָקִים וּבֵין הַשִּׁרְיָן.** בֵּין אוֹתָהּ רְצוּעָה שֶׁמַּדְבִּיקִין הַכּוֹבַע שֶׁעַל רֹאשׁוֹ לַשִּׁרְיוֹן. וְאֵין לוֹמַר מִצְרִי הָיָה, כִּי לְמִי נִתְכַּוֵּין? וַהֲלֹא מֶלֶךְ אֲרָם צִוָּה: **לֹא תִּלָּחֲמוּ אֶת הַקָּטֹן וְאֶת הַגָּדוֹל כִּי אִם אֶת מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל לְבַדּוֹ**? וְאַף לוֹ לֹא נִתְכַּוֵּין, שֶׁהֲרֵי כְּתִיב **לְתֻמּוֹ**; אֶלָּא בְּעַל כָּרְחוֹ יֵשׁ לוֹמַר: יִשְׂרָאֵל הָיָה הַמּוֹרֶה, וְנִתְכַּוֵּין לִירוֹת בְּמַחֲנֵה אֲרָם, וּלְפִי תֻמּוֹ הִכָּה **אֶת מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל**:


**between the scales and the coat of mail** between the thong with which they attach the helmet on his head to the coat of mail. We cannot say that he was a heathen, because whom was he aiming at? Did not the king of Aram command, “Attack neither small nor great, except the king of Israel”? And he did not even aim at him, for it is written, “innocently.” Perforce, you must say that the archer was an Israelite who intended to shoot at the camp of Aram, and inadvertently hit the king of Israel.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:33:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:4)


**וַיֹּאמֶר לָרַכָּב.** לַמַּנְהִיג אֶת הַמֶּרְכָּבָה:


**and he said to the charioteer** to the one who drives the chariot.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:33:5

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:5](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:33:5)


**וְהוֹצֵאתַנִי מִן הַמַּחֲנֶה.** כְּלוֹמַר: מִן הַמַּעֲרָכָה הַזּוֹ הָרִאשׁוֹנָה, וְהוֹשִׁיבֵנִי בַּמַּעֲרָכוֹת הָאַחֲרוֹנוֹת:


**and carry me out of the camp** i.e., away from these front lines, and take me to the rear lines.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:34:1)


**וַתַּעַל הַמִּלְחָמָה.** נִסְתַּלְּקָה, וְלֹא הוּזַק אֶחָד מִיִּשְׂרָאֵל כִּי אִם **מֶלֶךְ יִשְׂרָאֵל** לְבַדּוֹ, כִּדְבַר הַנָּבִיא שֶׁאָמַר: **לֹא אֲדוֹנִים לָאֵלֶּה יָשׁוּבוּ אִישׁ לְבֵיתוֹ בְּשָׁלוֹם** – לְשָׁלוֹם לֹא נֶאֱמַר, כִּי נֶהֱרַג אַחְאָב:


**And the battle withdrew** It withdrew, and not one of Israel was hurt except the king of Isra el, in accordance with the word of the prophet, (above verse 16): “These have no master. Let them return each one to his house in peace.” It does not say לְשָלוֹם, because Ahab was slain.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 18:34:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:34:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/18:34:2)


**מַעֲמִיד בַּמֶּרְכָּבָה.** הֶחֱזִיק וְעָמַד בַּמֶּרְכָּבָה, שֶׁלֹּא יָבִינוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל מַכָּתוֹ וְיִבְרְחוּ, וּתְחִלַּת נְפִילָה נִיסָה:


**was standing in the chariot** He exerted himself and stood up so that the Israelites should not realize that he was wounded and flee, for the beginning of defeat is fleeing.



## Chapter 19


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:3:1)


**כִּי בִעַרְתָּ הָאֲשֵׁרוֹת.** כְּדִלְעֵיל ״וְעוֹד הֵסִיר אֶת הַבָּמוֹת וְגוֹ׳״ (דברי הימים ב יז:ו):


**for you have abolished the asheroth** as above (17:6), “and he removed the high places [and the asherim], etc.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:3:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:3:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:3:2)


**לִדְרֹשׁ הָאֱלֹהִים.** כְּדִלְעֵיל ״כִּי לֵאלֹהֵי אָבִיו וְגוֹ׳״ (דברי הימים ב יז:ד):


**to seek God** as above (17:4): “but he inquired of the God of his father, etc.”



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:5:1)


**וַיַּעֲמֵד שׁוֹפְטִים.** כְּשֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַחוֹזֶה **דְּבָרִים טוֹבִים נִמְצְאוּ עִמָּךְ כִּי בִעַרְתָּ הָאֲשֵׁרוֹת**, בָּא לוֹ אֵצֶל סֵפֶר הַתּוֹרָה אִם בִּעֲרָם כַּמִּשְׁפָּט; וּמִתְּחִלָּה רָאָה כְתִיב ״אַבֵּד תְּאַבְּדוּן״ (דברים יב:ב) עַד ״וַאֲשֵׁרֵיהֶם תִּשְׂרְפוּן בָּאֵשׁ וּפְסִילֵי אֱלֹהֵיהֶם תְּגַדֵּעוּן״ (דברים יב:ג), וְנִזְכַּר שֶׁכָּתוּב בְּכִי תִשָּׂא: ״וְאֶת מַצֵּבֹתָם תְּשַׁבֵּרוּן וְגוֹ׳״ (שמות לד:יג). תָּמַהּ בְּעַצְמוֹ עַל שֶׁהֵיטִיב בְּעֵינֵי ה׳ שֶׁבִּעֵר הָאֲשֵׁרִים, אֲשֵׁירֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל, שֶׁהֲרֵי לֹא הִזְהִירָם אֶלָּא עַל אֲשֵׁירֵיהֶם, וְשׁוּב חָזַר בְּשׁוֹפְטִים וְשׁוֹטְרִים וּמָצָא כָתוּב ״לֹא תִטַּע לְךָ אֲשֵׁרָה״ (דברים טז:כא), וְאָז שָׂמַח שֶׁבִּעֲרָם. וְגַם רָאָה שָׁם כָּתוּב ״שֹׁפְטִים וְשֹׁטְרִים תִּתֶּן לְךָ״ (דברים טז:יח), מִיָּד וַיַּעֲמֵד שֹׁפְטִים מֻחְזָקִים **בְּכָל עָרֵי יְהוּדָה לְעִיר וָעִיר**:


**And he appointed judges** When the seer told him, “good things were found in you, for you abolished the asheroth,” he approached the Sepher Torah [to see] whether he had abolished them properly. He first saw that it was written (Deut. 12:2f.): “You shall utterly destroy...and you shall burn their asherim with fire and cut down the images of their gods,” and he remembered that it is written in כִּי תִשָׂא (Exod. 34:13): “And you shall break down their monuments, etc.” He wondered why he had pleased the Lord by abolishing the asherim, the Jewish asherim, for [the Torah] only warned about their asherim. He went back to [the Torah portion] (Deut. 16:18): “Judges and law enforcing agents,” and found written (verse 21): “You shall not plant an asherah.” Then he rejoiced that he had abolished them. He also saw written there: “Judges and law enforcing agents you shall appoint for yourself,” and immediately “he appointed judges,” strong ones.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:5:2)


**בְּכָל עָרֵי יְהוּדָה הַבְּצֻרוֹת.** הֶעֱמִיד מוּחְזָקִים, לְהַכְרִיחָם וּלְכוֹפָן לַדִּין מִתְּחִלָּה; וְשׁוּב הֶעֱמִיד שׁוֹפְטִים שֶׁאֵינָן מוּחְזָקִים כָּל כָּךְ לַעֲיָירוֹת שֶׁאֵינָן בְּצוּרוֹת, לְפִי שֶׁהֵן יְרֵאִים לִהְיוֹת סַרְבָנִים יוֹתֵר מֵאוֹתָן הַיּוֹשְׁבִים בְּעָרִים בְּצוּרוֹת, שֶׁבּוֹטְחִים בְּעָרֵי מִבְצָרָם:


**in all the fortified cities of Judah, for every city** He first appointed strong [judges] in all the fortified cities of Judah to coerce them [the people] and compel them to [accept] judgment. He later appointed judges who were not as strong for the cities that were not fortified, for they [the inhabitants] were more afraid to rebel than those who dwelt in the fortified cities, for they felt secure in their fortified cities.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:6:1)


**כִּי לֹא לְאָדָם תִּשְׁפְּטוּ.** פִּתְרוֹן: אַל תַּחְשְׁבוּ בִּלְבַבְכֶם לוֹמַר: מַה לָּנוּ אִם אָנוּ מַטִּים אֶת הַדִּין לְזַכּוֹת אֶת אוֹהֲבֵינוּ, לְהַטּוֹת דִּין דַּלִּים וְלִשָּׂא פְּנֵי עֲשִׁירִים, הֲלֹא אֵין הַמִּשְׁפָּט לַה׳; לְכָךְ נֶאֱמַר **לַה׳**, שֶׁלּוֹ הוּא, אִם חִיַּבְתָּ אֶת הַזַּכַּאי, כְּאִלּוּ נָטַלְתָּ וְלָקַחְתָּ מִשֶּׁל בּוֹרְאֲךָ וְדִין שָׁמַיִם עִקַּלְתָּ לָתֵת מִשְׁפָּט מְעֻקָּל. לְפִיכָךְ תִּרְאוּ מַה שֶּׁתַּעֲשׂוּ, וְהָיָה לְבַבְכֶם בְּכָל דִּין וְדִין כְּאִלּוּ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא עוֹמֵד לִפְנֵיכֶם בַּדִּין, וְזֶהוּ **וְעִמָּכֶם בִּדְבַר מִשְׁפָּט. וּבְסַנְהֶדְרִין (סנהדרין ו:) מְפֹרָשׁ <b>מָה אַתֶּם עוֹשִׂים? כִּי לֹא לְאָדָם תִּשְׁפְּטוּ כִּי לַה׳**; וְשֶׁמָּא יֹאמַר הַדַּיָּן: מַה לִּי וְלַצַּעַר הַזֶּה, תַּלְמוּד לוֹמַר: **וְעִמָּכֶם בִּדְבַר מִשְׁפָּט**, כְּלוֹמַר מוּטָל עֲלֵיכֶם לַעֲשׂוֹת וְאֵין לוֹ לַדַּיָּן לַעֲשׂוֹת אֶלָּא מַה שֶּׁעֵינָיו רוֹאוֹת:


**for you do not judge for man** The meaning is: Do not think in your heart: What difference does it make if we pervert the law to declare our friends innocent and pervert the rights of the poor and favor the rich? Is it not so that the judgment is not for the Lord? Therefore, it says, “for the Lord.” It is for Him. If you convict the innocent, it is as though you take from your Creator’s property and pervert the law of Heaven, by bestowing a distorted verdict. Therefore, “see what you are doing,” and may your heart be in every judgment, as if the Holy One, blessed be He, is standing before you in judgment, and this is the meaning of: “and [He] is with you in the matter of the judgment.” In *Sanhedrin* (6b) it is stated “What are you doing, for you do not judge for man but for the Lord.” This is explained [as follows]: Perhaps the judge will say, “How does this problem concern me?” Therefore, Scripture states: “and it is with you concerning the matter of the judgment,” i.e., it is incumbent upon you to rule justly. And the judge must deal only with that which he sees with his own eyes.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:7:1)


**אֵין עִם ה׳ אֱלֹהֵינוּ עַוְלָה וּמַשֹּׂא פָנִים וּמִקַּח שֹׁחַד.** וְהוּא **עִמָּכֶם** בַּמִּשְׁפָּט, לְפִיכָךְ אֵינוֹ רוֹצֶה שֶׁתַּעֲשׂוּ עָוֶל בַּמִּשְׁפָּט (ויקרא יט:טו):


**for with the Lord your God there is neither injustice, favoritism, nor bribe-taking** and He is with you in judgment; therefore, He does not wish you to commit injustice in judgment.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:8:1)


**וְגַם בִּירוּשָׁלִַם הֶעֱמִיד.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהוּא בְּעַצְמוֹ בִּירוּשָׁלִַם:


**And also in Jerusalem...set up judges** even though he himself was in Jerusalem.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:8:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:8:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:8:2)


**לְמִשְׁפַּט ה׳ וְלָרִיב.** מִשְׁפָּט נוֹפֵל לוֹמַר עַל דִּינֵי מָמוֹנוֹת, דּוּגְמָא: ״וְהָיְתָה לִבְנֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל לְחֻקַּת מִשְׁפָּט״ (במדבר כז:יא); וְלָרִיב נוֹפֵל עַל דִּינֵי נְפָשׁוֹת וְחַבָּלוֹת, דּוּגְמַת ״וְכִי יְרִיבֻן אֲנָשִׁים וְגוֹ׳״ (שמות כא:יח):


**for the judgment of the Lord and for quarrels** It is appropriate to use the term “judgment” (לְמִשְׁפַּט) concerning money matters, an example being (Num. 27:11) “and it shall be for the Children of Israel as a legal norm (לְחֻקַּת מִשְׁפָּט).” “And for quarrels” (וְלָרִיב) refers to capital cases and bodily injuries, an example being (Exod. 21:18) “And if men quarrel, etc.” (יְרִיבוּן).



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:9:1)


**כֹּה תַעֲשׂוּן.** מִשּׁוּם זְרִיזוּת וּזְהִירוּת וּלְחוֹזֶק הוֹסִיף נוּ״ן, כְּמוֹ ״עַד אֲשֶׁר תֵּדְעִין אֵיךְ יִפֹּל דָּבָר״ (רות ג:יח), וּכְמוֹ ״כֹּה תֹאמְרוּן לַאדֹנִי לְעֵשָׂו״ (בראשית לב:ה); ״וְכֹה תִדְבָּקִין וְגוֹ׳״ (רות ב:ח); ״אֲשֶׁר יִקְצֹרוּן״ (רות ב:ט):


**So shall you do** Because of alacrity, caution, and encouragement, he added a “nun,” like (Gen. 32: 5): “So shall you say (תאמְרוּן),” and like (Ruth 2:8f.): “and here you shall stay (תִדְבָּקִין) with my maidens. Your eyes shall be on the field that they reap (יִִִקְצרוּן),” and like (ibid. 3:18): “until you know (תֵּדְעִין) how the matter will be resolved, for the man will not rest, etc.”



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:1)


**בֵּין דָּם לְדָם.** בֵּין דַּם רְצִיחַת שׁוֹגֵג לְמֵזִיד:


**between blood[shed] and blood[shed]** between the bloodshed of an unintentional murder and an intentional one.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:2)


**וְהִזְהַרְתֶּם אוֹתָם.** אֲחֵיכֶם:


**you shall admonish them** your brethren.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:3)


**וְהָיָה קֶצֶף עֲלֵיכֶם וְעַל אֲחֵיכֶם.** לְפִיכָךְ **כֹּה תַעֲשׂוּן: וְהִזְהַרְתֶּם** אֶת אֲחֵיכֶם:


**and there be wrath upon you and upon your brethren** Therefore **so shall you do** You shall admonish your brethren.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:10:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:10:4)


**וְלֹא תֶאְשָׁמוּ.** אוֹתָם מִכָּל מָקוֹם, שֶׁאִם תְּקַבְּלוּ אֶת הָאַזְהָרָה לֹא יִהְיֶה לָכֶם **קֶצֶף**, וְאַף לֹא יִהְיֶה בְּשׁוּם דָּבָר אָשָׁם עֲלֵיכֶם, הוֹאִיל וַעֲשִׂיתֶם חוֹבַתְכֶם הַמּוּטָל עֲלֵיכֶם לַעֲשׂוֹת:


**and you will not be guilty** In any case, you [will not be guilty], for if you accept the admonition, there will be no wrath upon you, and there will also be no guilt upon you in anything since you performed the duty which was incumbent upon you to perform.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 19:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/19:11:1)


**וִיהִי ה׳ עִם הַטּוֹב.** עִם עוֹשֵׂה הַטּוֹב וְהַיָּשָׁר (דברים יב:כח):


**and may the Lord be with the good** with the one who performs that which is good and upright.



## Chapter 20


### Verse 1

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:1:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:1:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:1:1)


**וְעִמָּהֶם מֵהָעַמּוֹנִים.** זֶה עֲמָלֵק, וְלָמָּה נִקְרְאוּ עַמּוֹנִים? לְפִי שֶׁשִּׁנָּה כְּסוּתוֹ וּלְשׁוֹנוֹ כְּעַמּוֹנִים וְנִתְעָרְבוּ בָּהֶם, וּבָא לְהִלָּחֵם בְּיִשְׂרָאֵל (ילקוט שמעוני תורה תשסד); וְכֵן מוּכָח לְמַטָּה בָּעִנְיָן **וּבְעֵת הַחֵלּוּ בְּרִנָּה וּתְהִלָּה נָתַן ה׳ מְאָרְבִים עַל בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן וּמוֹאָב וְהַר שֵׂעִיר וְגוֹ׳** – נִמְצָא שֶׁהָעַמּוֹנִים שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר כָּאן הֵם עֲמָלֵקִים שֶׁשִּׁנּוּ לְשׁוֹנָם וּכְסוּתָם. וְהוּא שֶׁיִּסֵּד הַקַּלִּיר בְּפָרָשַׁת זָכוֹר (בפיוט זכור איש): ״זְכוֹר עָרִיץ בָּעַמּוֹנִים נֶחְבָּא, פָּרַץ כַּרְמֵי עֵין גֶּדִי כְּהוּבָא״ – כְּלוֹמַר: זְכוֹר מַה שֶּׁעָשָׂה עֲמָלֵק (דברים כה:יז) הֶעָרִיץ שֶׁנֶּחְבָּא בֵּין הָעַמּוֹנִים, וּבָא לִפְרֹץ כַּרְמֵי יִשְׂרָאֵל בְּאוֹתוֹ זְמַן, כְּדִכְתִיב ״וְהִנָּם בְּחַצְצוֹן תָּמָר הִיא עֵין גֶּדִי״ (דברי הימים ב כ:ב). וְ״עֵין גֶּדִי״ סָמוּךְ לִמְחוֹז שֶׁל עֲמָלֵק, כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר בְּלֶךְ לְךָ ״וַיַּכּוּ אֶת כָּל שְׂדֵה הָעֲמָלֵקִי וְגַם אֶת הָאֱמֹרִי הַיּוֹשֵׁב בְּחַצְצוֹן תָּמָר״ (בראשית יד:ז):


**and with them, some of the Ammonites** This is Amalek. Why were they called Ammonites? Because they changed their dress and their language to resemble that of the Ammonites and mingled with them, and came to wage war against Israel. This can be proven below in the chapter (verse 22): “And at the time they commenced, etc. the Lord placed liers-in-wait against the children of Ammon, Moab, and Mount Seir.” We find that the Ammonites mentioned here are Amalekites who changed their tongue and their dress. This is what the Kallir set down in the section of Zachor: Remember the tyrant who hid among the Ammonites; he breached the vineyards of Ein-Gedi when he was brought, i.e., remember what Amalekite the tyrant did - he hid among the Ammonites and came to breach the vineyards of Israel at that time, as it is written: “and behold they are in Hazazon-Tamar which is Ein-Gedi,” and Ein-Gedi is close to the territory of Amalek, as is stated in (Gen. 14:7): “and they struck down the whole field of the Amalekites, and also the Amorites who dwell in Hazazon- Tamar.”



### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:2:1)


**הִיא עֵין גֶּדִי.** כְּלוֹמַר, שֶׁאָמְרוּ לוֹ: כְּבָר בָּאוּ כָּל כָּךְ בְּקָרוֹב לָנוּ:


**the same is Ein-Gedi** i.e., They said to him, “They have already come so close to us.”



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:4:1)


**גַּם מִכָּל עָרֵי יְהוּדָה.** שֶׁלֹּא תֹאמַר: כְּשֶׁאָמַר בִּתְחִלָּה **וַיִּקָּבְצוּ יְהוּדָה לְבַקֵּשׁ** – אֵלּוּ יוֹשְׁבֵי יְרוּשָׁלַיִם לְבַד הֵם, אֶלָּא מִכָּל עָרֵי יְהוּדָה **בָּאוּ**:


**also from all the cities of Judah** That you should not say, when it is stated initially, “And the Judeans gathered to seek,” that these were the inhabitants of Jerusalem only, but they came from all the cities of Judah.



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:1)


**וְאַתָּה מוֹשֵׁל בְּכָל מַמְלְכוֹת הַגּוֹיִם וּבְיָדְךָ כֹּחַ וּגְבוּרָה.** לִנְהוֹג מֶמְשַׁלְתְּךָ עֲלֵיהֶם, לְכָךְ אָנוּ מְבַקְּשִׁים מִמְּךָ לְעָזְרֵנוּ מֵהֶם:


**and that You rule over all the kingdoms of the nations and in Your hand is strength and might** to conduct Your rule over them; therefore, we beseech You to save us from them.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:2)


**וּבְיָדְךָ כֹּחַ.** בַּעַל כֹּחַ וּגְבוּרָה; הַרְדִי״ן בְּלַעַ״ז:


**and in Your hand is strength and might** in wars and might, *hardiess* in French, boldness.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:6:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:6:3)


**וְאֵין עִמְּךָ לְהִתְיַצֵּב.** אֵין שׁוּם בְּרִיָּה יְכוֹלָה לַעֲמֹד נֶגְדְּךָ **בְּכָל מַמְלְכוֹת הַגּוֹיִם**; דּוּגְמָתוֹ: ״לֹא יִתְיַצֵּב אִישׁ לְפָנֶיךָ״ (יהושע א:ה), וְכֵן (איוב מא:ב) ״מִי הוּא לְפָנַי יִתְיַצֵּב״ (כָּךְ פֵּירֵשׁ רַבֵּינוּ מְנַחֵם בַּר חֶלְבּוֹ זַצַ״ל):


**and no one can stand with You** No creature can stand against You of all the kingdoms of the nations, and an example of this is (Josh. 1:5): “No man shall stand before You.” (Rabbi Menahem the son of Helbo explained it in this manner.)



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:7:1)


**וַתִּתְּנֶהָ לְזֶרַע אַבְרָהָם.** לְפִיכָךְ עָלֶיךָ לְהַחֲזִיקָהּ בְּיָדָם וּלְגָרֵשׁ הָעַמִּים הָאֵלֶּה, כִּי אֲפִלּוּ מֶלֶךְ וַאֲפִלּוּ שׁוּם אָדָם כְּשֶׁנָּתַן מַתָּנָה לְאוֹהֲבוֹ וּבָא אַחֵר לְגָזְלוֹ הֵימֶנּוּ, דּוֹחֵק בְּעַצְמוֹ לְהַחֲזִיקָהּ בְּיָדוֹ, כָּל שֶׁכֵּן אַתָּה. וְשֶׁמָּא תֹּאמַר לֹא נְתַתִּיהָ לָכֶם אֶלָּא לְפִי שָׁעָה, בִּשְׁבִיל כָּךְ נֶאֱמַר לְעֵיל יְרֻשַּׁת עוֹלָם נָתַתָּ לָנוּ:


**and given it to the seed of Abraham** Therefore, it is incumbent upon You to preserve it in their hands and to drive these peoples out. Even a flesh and blood king, and even an ordinary man, when he gives a present to his friend and someone comes to rob him of it, he exerts himself to preserve it in his hand. How much more so [should] You [do so]! Perhaps You will say, “I gave it to them only temporarily.” Therefore, it says, “forever.” You gave it to us for an eternal inheritance.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:8:1)


**וַיִּבְנוּ לְךָ בָהּ מִקְדָּשׁ לְשִׁמְךָ לֵאמֹר.** שֶׁ**אִם תָּבוֹא עָלֵינוּ רָעָה חֶרֶב שְׁפוֹט** אוֹ שׁוּם דִּין **וְדֶבֶר וְרָעָב**:


**and therein they built You a Sanctuary for Your name, saying** that “should evil come upon us, etc.” **judgment** or any judgment, whether sword, pestilence, or famine.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:9:1)


**וְנִזְעַק אֵלֶיךָ מִצָּרָתֵנוּ.** בְּבֵית הַמִּקְדָּשׁ:


**and we shall cry out to You from our distress** in the Temple.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:9:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:9:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:9:2)


**וְתִשְׁמַע וְתוֹשִׁיעַ.** כַּאֲשֶׁר הִתְפַּלֵּל לְפָנֶיךָ שְׁלֹמֹה, וְהִבְטַחְתּוֹ לַעֲשׂוֹת כָּךְ; כְּמוֹ שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר ״וְאִם יִנָּגֵף עַמְּךָ״ (דברי הימים ב ו:כד) וְכָל הַפָּרָשָׁה עַד ״וְסָלַחְתָּ וְנָתַתָּ לָאִישׁ כְּכָל דְּרָכָיו״ (דברי הימים ב ו:ל). וַיֵּעָתֶר לוֹ ה׳, כְּדִכְתִיב ״וַיֵּרָא ה׳ אֶל שְׁלֹמֹה וַיֹּאמֶר לוֹ שָׁמַעְתִּי אֶת תְּפִלָּתֶךָ״ (דברי הימים ב ז:יב) וְכָל הַפָּרָשָׁה עַד ״וְאֶסְלַח לְחַטָּאתָם וְאֶרְפָּא אֶת אַרְצָם״ (דברי הימים ב ז:יד). וְעַתָּה אֲנִי מִתְפַּלֵּל שֶׁתִּזְכֹּר אוֹתָהּ הַבְטָחָה שֶׁהִבְטַחְתּוֹ, וְתִשְׁמַע וְתוֹשִׁיעֵנוּ מֵאוֹיְבֵינוּ הַבָּאִים עָלֵינוּ:


**and You will hear and save** as Solomon prayed before You, and You promised him to do so, as is written (above 6:24): “And if Your people is struck down,” continuing with the entire chapter until (verse 30): “and forgive and give to each man according to his ways.” And the Lord accepted his prayer, as it is written (ibid. 7:12): “And the Lord appeared to Solomon, and said to him: I have heard your prayer,” continuing with the entire chapter until (ibid. verse 14) “and forgive their sin and heal their land.” Now I pray that You remember the promise which You promised him, and hear and save us from our enemies who are coming upon us.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:1)


**וְעַתָּה.** עַל כָּל זֹאת שֶׁהִבְטַחְתָּנוּ לְהוֹשִׁיעֵנוּ מֵאוֹיְבֵינוּ הַבָּאִים עָלֵינוּ:


**And now** about all that You promised us to save us from our enemies who are coming upon us.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:2)


**הִנֵּה בְנֵי עַמּוֹן וּמוֹאָב וְהַר שֵׂעִיר אֲשֶׁר לֹא נָתַתָּה לְיִשְׂרָאֵל לָבוֹא בָהֶם.** לְהִלָּחֵם:


**behold the children of Ammon and Moab and Mount Seir, against whom You did not permit Israel to come** to wage war.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:10:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:10:3)


**בְּבוֹאָם מֵאֶרֶץ מִצְרַיִם.** וַעֲלֵיהֶם הָיָה לִזְכֹּר אֶת הַחֶסֶד וְלָבֹא בְּעֶזְרָתֵנוּ, וְהֵם לֹא דַי לָהֶם שֶׁאֵינָן זוֹכְרִין לָנוּ אוֹתוֹ הַחֶסֶד לִהְיוֹת עִמָּנוּ בְּכָל צָרוֹתֵינוּ, אֶלָּא שֶׁגּוֹמְלִין אֵלֵינוּ רָעָה וּבָאִין עָלֵינוּ:


**when they came out of the land of Egypt** and they should have remembered the kindness [we performed] and come to our aid, but they - not only did they not remember our kindness, to be with us in all our troubles, but they repayed us with evil and attacked us.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:11:1)


**לְגָרְשֵׁנוּ מִיְּרֻשָּׁתְךָ אֲשֶׁר הוֹרַשְׁתָּנוּ.** אַתָּה בְּעַצְמְךָ, וּלְפִיכָךְ מַה יֵּשׁ לָהֶם לְעַרְעֵר עַל יְרֻשָּׁתֵנוּ:


**to drive us out of Your inheritance, which You caused us to inherit** You Yourself. Therefore, on what basis can they contest our inheritance?



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:12:1)


**כִּי עָלֶיךָ עֵינֵינוּ.** לְהוֹשִׁיעֵנוּ:


**but our eyes are upon You** to save us.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:13:1)


**גַּם טַפָּם.** כַּמִּנְהָג שֶׁהַגְּדוּדִים בָּאִים עַל הָאָרֶץ, בּוֹרְחִים מִן הַכְּפָרִים לְעָרֵי מִבְצְרֵיהֶם טַפָּם **נְשֵׁיהֶם וּבְנֵיהֶם**:


**also their infants** It is customary that when troops attack a country, the infants, wives, and children flee the villages to the fortified cities.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:15:1)


**אַל תִּירְאוּ וְאַל תֵּחַתּוּ.** מוּסָב עַל שֶׁאָמְרוּ **אֵין בָּנוּ כֹּחַ לִפְנֵי הֶהָמוֹן**, וְהַנָּבִיא אָמַר לָהֶם: אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהֵם רַבִּים מִכֶּם, אַל תִּירְאוּ מֵהֶם:


**You shall not fear, neither shall you be dismayed** This refers back to what they said, (verse 12) “we have no strength before this multitude.” The prophet said to them, “Although they outnumber you, do not fear them.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:15:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:15:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:15:2)


**כִּי לֹא לָכֶם הַמִּלְחָמָה כִּי לֵאלֹהִים.** מוּסָב עַל **וַאֲנַחְנוּ לֹא נֵדַע**, וְהֵשִׁיב הַנָּבִיא: וַדַּאי כַּאֲשֶׁר אֲמַרְתֶּם שֶׁעַל הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא עֵינֵיכֶם וּבִטְחוֹנְכֶם לְהוֹשִׁיעֲכֶם, כֵּן יַעֲשֶׂה, כִּי לֹא לָכֶם הַמִּלְחָמָה כִּי לֵאלֹהִים:


**for the war is not yours but God’s** This refers back to (verse 12) “and we do not know,” and the prophet replied, “It is surely as you have said, that your eyes and your trust are upon the Holy One, blessed be He, to save you.” So will He do, for the war is not yours but God’s.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:16:1)


**בְּמַעֲלֵה הַצִּיץ.** שֵׁם מָקוֹם:


**on the ascent to Haziz** the name of a place.



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:1)


**הִתְיַצְּבוּ.** מוּסָב עַל **לֹא לָכֶם הַמִּלְחָמָה**:


**set yourself** This refers back to (verse 15) “for the war is not yours.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:17:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:2)


**וְרָאוּ אֶת יְשׁוּעַת ה׳.** מוּסָב לְמַעְלָה: הֲלֹא אַתָּה אֱלֹהֵינוּ **הוֹרַשְׁתָּנוּ**, וְאָמַר לָהֶם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא: הַדִּין עִמָּכֶם, עָלַי לְהַחֲזִיקָהּ בְּיֶדְכֶם:


**and see the salvation of the Lord** This refers back to the above: “Have You, our God, not caused us to inherit (sic)?” And the Holy One, blessed be He, said to them, “You are right. It is up to Me to preserve it in your hands.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:17:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:17:3)


**מָחָר צְאוּ.** וְאֶלָּחֵם בַּעֲדֵיכֶם. וְהֵם הֶאֱמִינוּ וְנִזְדָּרְזוּ וַיַּשְׁכִּימוּ בַּבֹּקֶר:


**Tomorrow go forth before them** and I shall wage war on your behalf. And they believed, and hurried, and arose early in the morning.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:20:1)


**וְתֵאָמֵנוּ.** וְתִהְיוּ בְּנֵי אֱמוּנָה וְתַצְלִיחוּ; דֻּגְמָא: ״לֹא תֵאָמֵנוּ״ (ישעיה ז:ט):


**and you will be believed** Heb. וְתֵאָמֵנוּ. You will be trustworthy and prosper. An example is (Isa. 7: 9) “for you cannot be believed (לֹא תֵאָמֵנוּ).”



### Verse 22

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:22:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:22:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:22:1)


**נָתַן ה׳ מְאָרְבִים.** נָתַן מְאָרְבִים לְהַשְׁמִידָם וּלְהַחֲרִימָם; דּוּגְמָא בְּאֲבִיָּה בֶּן רְחַבְעָם: ״וְיָרָבְעָם הֵסֵב אֶת הַמַּאֲרָב״ (דברי הימים ב יג:יג); כְּמוֹ כֵן הֱסִיבָם הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא זֶה בָּזֶה, וְשׁוּב חָזַר **מוֹאָב** וְעָזַר לִבְנֵי **עַמּוֹן**, וְקָמוּ שְׁנֵיהֶם עַל בְּנֵי **שֵׂעִיר**:


**the Lord placed liers-in-wait** He placed liers-in-wait to annihilate them and destroy them. An example is found in the case of Abijah the son of Rehoboam (13:13): “And Jeroboam turned the ambush.” Similarly, the Holy One, blessed be He, turned them one against another, and later, Moab returned and aided the children of Ammon, and they both rose up against the children of Seir.



### Verse 23

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:23:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:23:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:23:1)


**וַיַּעַמְדוּ בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן וּמוֹאָב עַל יֹשְׁבֵי הַר שֵׂעִיר לְהַחֲרִים וּלְהַשְׁמִיד וּכְכַלּוֹתָם בְּיוֹשְׁבֵי שֵׂעִיר עָזְרוּ אִישׁ בְּרֵעֵהוּ.** הֱסִיבָן הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא זֶה אֶל זֶה לְהַשְׁחִיתָם עַד כַּלּוֹתָם, עַד שֶׁנָּפְלוּ הַהַגְרִים (תהלים פג:ז) לָאָרֶץ לְאֵין פְּלֵיטָה:


**when they finished with the inhabitants of Seir, each one helped his friend to destroy** The Holy One, blessed be He, turned one against the other until they were completely destroyed; until the corpses fell to the ground with no survivors.



### Verse 25

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:25:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:25:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:25:1)


**לְאֵין מַשָּׂא.** מֵרוֹב:


**to the extent that they could not carry them away** because of the quantity.



### Verse 26

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:26:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:26:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:26:1)


**נִקְהֲלוּ לְעֵמֶק בְּרָכָה.** לְאוֹתוֹ עֵמֶק שֶׁלְּאַחַר כֵּן נִקְרָא עֵמֶק בְּרָכָה, עַל שֵׁם הַמַּעֲשֶׂה, כְּמוֹ שֶׁמְּפֹרָשׁ בְּסָמוּךְ, כְּדַאֲמַר: **כִּי שָׁם בֵּרְכוּ אֶת ה׳**; וְהוֹדוּ עַל כָּל הַטּוֹבָה אֲשֶׁר עָשָׂה לָהֶם, שֶׁעֲזָרָם מֵעוֹבְדֵי כּוֹכָבִים וּמַזָּלוֹת:


**they congregated to the valley of Berachah** To the valley which was later called the valley of Berachah, because of the incident [that took place there], as is explained immediately following, as Scripture states **for there they blessed the Lord** and gave thanks for all the good that He had done for them, that He saved them from their enemies.



### Verse 28

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:28:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:28:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:28:1)


**וַיָּבֹאוּ יְרוּשָׁלִַם בִּנְבָלִים וּבְכִנֹּרוֹת וּבַחֲצֹצְרוֹת.** הֲרֵי שְׁלֹשָׁה כְּלֵי שִׁיר, כְּנֶגֶד שְׁלֹשָׁה: **כָּל אִישׁ יְהוּדָה וִירוּשָׁלִַם וִיהוֹשָׁפָט**:


**And they came to Jerusalem with psalteries, with harps, and with trumpets** These three musical instruments correspond to “all the men of Judah and Jerusalem and Jehoshaphat.”



### Verse 32

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:32:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:32:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:32:1)


**וַיֵּלֶךְ בְּדֶרֶךְ אָבִיו אָסָא.** מֵאֵיזֶה דָּבָר **לֹא סָר? מִלַּעֲשׂוֹת הַיָּשָׁר**; מִדֶּרֶךְ הַטּוֹב וְהַיָּשָׁר שֶׁל אָבִיו לֹא סָר, אֲבָל מִדַּרְכּוֹ הָרָעָה שֶׁל אָסָא סָר, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב ״וַיּוֹצֵא אָסָא כֶּסֶף וְזָהָב״ (דברי הימים ב טז:ב) וְכָל הַפָּרָשָׁה, אֲבָל יְהוֹשָׁפָט לֹא עָשָׂה כֵּן, אֶלָּא בָּטַח כָּל יָמָיו בַּהַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא:


**And he went in the way of his father Asa and did not turn away** From what did he not turn away? **to do what was right** From the good and upright way of his father he did not turn away, but from the bad ways of Asa he did turn away, as is written (above 16:2): “And Asa brought forth silver and gold,” and the entire incident, but Jehoshaphat did not do so, but trusted in the Holy One, blessed be He, all his life.



### Verse 34

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:34:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:34:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:34:1)


**בְּדִבְרֵי יֵהוּא בֶן חֲנָנִי.** שֶׁאָמַר אֶל הַמֶּלֶךְ יְהוֹשָׁפָט: ״הֲלָרָשָׁע לַעֲזֹר״ (דברי הימים ב יט:ב):


**in the words of Jehu the son of Hanani** who said to King Jehoshaphat, (19:2) “[Did you go] to aid the wicked?”



### Verse 37

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 20:37:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:37:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/20:37:1)


**וְלֹא עָצְרוּ.** פִּתְרוֹן: לֹא עָצְרוּ כֹּחַ הָ**אֳנִיּוֹת** לָלֶכֶת **וַיִּשָּׁבְרוּ**:


**and could not go** - Heb. וְלֹא עָצְרוּ, lit., they did not hold in. The meaning is: The ships could not muster the strength to go, and they were broken.



## Chapter 21


### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:7:1)


**נִיר.** פֵּירוּשׁ: עוֹל עַל יִשְׂרָאֵל:


**a kingdom** - Heb. נִיר. Its meaning is a yoke over Israel.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:8:1)


**בִּימָיו פָּשַׁע אֱדוֹם וגו׳.** כִּי דָוִד הִכְרִית כָּל זָכָר בֶּאֱדוֹם (מלכים א יא:טו-טז), וַיִּתֵּן בָּהֶם נְצִיבִים בִּמְקוֹם מֶלֶךְ (שמואל ב ח:יד), וּפָרְעוּ לוֹ מַס. וְכֵן הָיוּ עוֹשִׂין כָּל מַלְכֵי יְהוּדָה: נוֹתְנִין הַנְּצִיבִים הַטּוֹבִים בְּעֵינֵיהֶם (מלכים א כב:מח). וּמִימָיו שֶׁל יְהוֹרָם וָאֵילָךְ פָּשְׁעוּ **מִתַּחַת יַד יְהוּדָה**:


**In his days, Edom rebelled, etc.** For David had destroyed all the males in Edom, and he appointed governors over them instead of a king, and they paid him tribute, and all the kings of Judah did similarly: they appointed governors who pleased them. And from the days of Joram and thereafter, they [the Edomites] rebelled against the authority of Judah.



### Verse 9

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:9:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:9:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:9:1)


**הַסּוֹבֵב אֵלָיו. וַיַּךְ** אוֹתוֹ **אֱדוֹם** הַסּוֹבְבִים וְסוֹמְכִים לוֹ לְעָזְרוֹ; כִּי הָיָה דַּרְכָּם, כְּשֶׁנּוֹצְחִים בַּמִּלְחָמָה הָיוּ תּוֹפְשִׂים הָאֲנָשִׁים הַגִּבּוֹרִים וּמְבִיאִים אוֹתָן עִמָּהֶם, שֶׁלֹּא יִתְוַסְּפוּ עַל שׂוֹנְאֵיהֶם לִהְיוֹת לְעֶזְרָה לֶאֱדוֹם בְּנֵי עַמָּם; וְקָם לַיְלָה פִּתְאוֹם וְהָרְגָם. וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ פֵּרְשׁוּ **הַסּוֹבֵב אֵלָיו** – סְמוּכִים לְפָנָיו:


**who surrounded him** He smote those Edomites who surrounded him and supported him to aid him, for it was their custom that when they were victorious in war, they would seize the mighty men and bring them with them lest they join their enemies to aid the Edomites, their countrymen. In this case, he got up at night suddenly and slew them. Our Sages, however, explained “those who surrounded him” as “those near him.”



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:10:1)


**וַיִּפְשַׁע אֱדוֹם.** וַהֲלֹא כְּבָר נֶאֱמַר לְמַעְלָה **בְּיָמָיו פָּשַׁע אֱדוֹם**! אֶלָּא כֵּן דֶּרֶךְ הַפָּסוּק, לְפִי שֶׁהִפְסִיק בֵּנְתַּיִם פָּסוּק אֶחָד, וְרוֹצֶה לוֹמַר: **אָז תִּפְשַׁע לִבְנָה**, לְכָךְ סִדְּרָן מִתְּחִלָּה. וְאוֹמֵר: וַיִּפְשַׁע אֱדוֹם, **אָז תִּפְשַׁע לִבְנָה.** שֶׁל יְהוּדָה הָיְתָה, וְהָיְתָה עוֹמֶדֶת עַל גְּבוּל אֱדוֹם, כְּדִכְתִיב בְּסֵפֶר יְהוֹשֻׁעַ ״וַיְהִי הֶעָרִים מִקְצֵה לְמַטֵּה בְּנֵי יְהוּדָה אֶל גְּבוּל אֱדוֹם״ (יהושע טו:כא), וּכְשֶׁרָאוּ שֶׁמָּרְדוּ אֲדוֹמִיִּים, מָרְדוּ אַף הֵם **בָּעֵת הַהִיא**, שֶׁיּוֹשְׁבִים עַל גְּבוּל אֱדוֹם וְעָזְרוּ לֶאֱדוֹם; וְזֶהוּ **אָז תִּפְשַׁע לִבְנָה**:


**And Edom rebelled** Was it not already stated above (verse 8): “In his days, Edom rebelled?” But this is the manner of Scripture: because a verse is interposed, and it now wishes to continue with “then Libnah rebelled,” it repeats the [original words] stating, “And Edom rebelled.” **then Libnah rebelled** It belonged to Judah, and it was situated on the border of Edom, as is written in the Book of Joshua (15:21, 42): “And the outermost cities of the tribe of the children of Judah toward the border of Edom.” When they saw that the Edomites rebelled, they too rebelled, for they lived on the border of Edom, and they aided Edom. This is [the meaning of] “then Libnah rebelled.”


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:10:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:10:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:10:2)


**כִּי עָזַב אֶת ה׳ אֱלֹהֵי אֲבוֹתָיו.** מַגִּיד מַה גָּרַם לוֹ שֶׁמָּרְדוּ בְיָמָיו יוֹתֵר מִשְּׁאָר מַלְכֵי יְהוּדָה שֶׁהָיוּ לְפָנָיו:


**because he had forsaken the Lord, the God of his forefathers** [Scripture] explains what it was that caused them to rebel in his time more than during [the time of] the rest of the kings of Judah who preceded him.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:11:1)


**וַיֶּזֶן.** כָּל מִי שֶׁעוֹזֵב וּמֵימֵר יִרְאַת הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בַּעֲבוֹדַת כּוֹכָבִים וּמַזָּלוֹת נוֹפֵל לְשׁוֹן זְנוּת שֶׁעָשָׂה, דּוּגְמַת אִשָּׁה מְזַנָּה וּמְנָאֶפֶת מֵאֲחֵרִים, וְדוֹמֶה ״כִּי זָנֹה תִזְנֶה הָאָרֶץ״ (הושע א:ב); ״וְזָנוּ אַחֲרֵי אֱלֹהֵי נֵכָר וְגוֹ׳״ (דברים לא:טז):


**and led the inhabitants of Jerusalem astray** - Heb. זְנוּת. It is appropriate to use the term harlotry in connection with anyone who forsakes and exchanges the fear of the Holy One, blessed be He, for the idolatry of star and planet worship - for such a person behaves in the manner of a promiscuous woman who commits adultery with others, and similar to this are (Deut. 31:16): “and this people will rise and go astray (וְזָנָה) after the foreign gods of the land”; (Hos. 1:2): “for the land will go astray (זָנה תִזְנֶה), etc.”



### Verse 12

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:12:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:12:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:12:1)


**וַיָּבֹא אֵלָיו מִכְתָּב מֵאֵלִיָּהוּ.** לְאַחַר שֶׁעָלָה לַשָּׁמַיִם בָּא לוֹ כְּתָב זֶה שֶׁל נְבוּאָה זוֹ:


**And a letter came to him from Elijah** After he had ascended to heaven, this prophetic letter came to him [i.e., to Joram].



### Verse 17

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:17:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:17:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:17:1)


**וַיִּבְקָעוּהָ.** כְּמוֹ ״נַעֲלֶה בִיהוּדָה וּנְקִיצֶנָּה וְנַבְקִיעֶנָּה״ (ישעיה ז:ו), וְכֵן ״לְהַבְקִיעַ אֶל מֶלֶךְ אֱדוֹם״ (מלכים ב ג:כו):


**and split it** - Heb. וַיִִּבְקָעוּהָ, like (Isa. 7:6): “Let us go up to Judah and provoke it and breach its walls וְנַבְקִעֶנָה and so, (II Kings 3:26): “to break a way (לְהַבְקִיעַ)o the king of Edom.”



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:19:1)


**וּכְעֵת צֵאת הַקֵּץ.** אוֹתוֹ קֵץ שֶׁאָמַר לוֹ הַנָּבִיא:


**and at the end** the end that the prophet had foretold him of.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:19:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:19:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:19:2)


**וְלֹא עָשׂוּ לוֹ עַמּוֹ שְׂרֵפָה.** אַף עַל פִּי שֶׁהָיָה צָרִיךְ לְאוֹתוֹ שְׂרֵפָה, לְפִי שֶׁחָלָה בָָָּחֳלָיִים **רָעִים** וּבְשָׂרוֹ מַסְרִיחַ, לֹא חָפֵץ הַקָּדוֹשׁ בָּרוּךְ הוּא בִּכְבוֹדוֹ שֶׁיַּעֲשׂוּ לוֹ שְׂרֵפָה, וְגַם **לֹא** נִקְבַּר **בְּקִבְרוֹת הַמְּלָכִים**:


**and his people did not make him a burning** even though he required that burning because he had been ailing with malignant illnesses, and his flesh reeked. The Holy One, blessed be He, did not wish to honor him by making a burning for him, and he was also not buried in the graves of the kings.



### Verse 20

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 21:20:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:20:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/21:20:1)


**בְּלֹא חֶמְדָּה.** פִּתְרוֹן: כָּל יָמָיו הָיָה בְּרָעָה וּבְתַחֲלוּאִים, וְעָלָיו נִתְקַיֵּים ״רְאֵה נָתַתִּי לְפָנֶיךָ הַיּוֹם אֶת הַחַיִּים וְגוֹ׳״ (דברים ל:טו), וְגַם ״קְצַר יָמִים וּשְׂבַע רֹגֶז״ (איוב יד:א):


**and he departed joyless** The meaning is: all his life he was in distress and in illnesses, and with him was fulfilled (Deut. 30:15): “See, I have set before you today life, etc.” and (Job 14:1) “short of days and full of fear.”

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:2:1)


**בֶּן אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁתַּיִם.** וּבִמְלָכִים הוּא אוֹמֵר ״בֶּן עֶשְׂרִים וּשְׁתַּיִם״ (מלכים ב ח:כו), הָא כֵּיצַד? לְפִי זֶה הָיָה הַבֵּן גָּדוֹל מֵאָבִיו שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים, אֶלָּא שְׁתֵּי שָׁנִים קֹדֶם שֶׁנּוֹלַד יְהוֹרָם אָבִיו נִגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה זוֹ עַל בֵּית דָּוִד שֶׁתִּכְלֶה עִם בֵּית אַחְאָב, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר **וּמֵאֱלֹהִים הָיְתָה תְבוּסַת אֲחַזְיָה וְגוֹ׳**; וּבְיוֹם שֶׁנָּשָׂא אָסָא אֶת בַּת עָמְרִי לִיהוֹשָׁפָט בְּנוֹ נִגְזְרָה גְּזֵרָה זוֹ. כָּךְ שְׁנוּיָה בְּסֵדֶר עוֹלָם (סדר עולם רבה יז) וּבְתוֹסֶפְתָּא דְסוֹטָה (סוטה יב:ג). אַךְ בְּכָל הַמִּקְרָאוֹת לֹא מָצִינוּ שֶׁנָּשָׂא יְהוֹשָׁפָט בַּת עָמְרִי, אַךְ מָצָאתִי ״וַיִּתְחַתֵּן לְאַחְאָב״ (דברי הימים ב יח:א), וְשֶׁמָּא זוֹ הִיא אֲחוֹתוֹ שֶׁנָּשָׂא. וּבִשְׁנַת שְׁלֹשִׁים וְאַחַת לְאָסָא נְשָׂאָהּ, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר ״וּבִשְׁנַת שְׁלֹשִׁים וְאַחַת לְאָסָא... מָלַךְ עָמְרִי״ (מלכים א טז:כג), וּכְשֶׁנָּשָׂא אָסָא אֶת בִּתּוֹ לִיהוֹשָׁפָט בְּנוֹ, שֶׁנִּתְעַלָּה עָמְרִי וְהָרְגוֹ תִּבְנִי, צֵא וַחֲשֹׁב מִשְּׁנַת שְׁלֹשִׁים וְשָׁלֹשׁ לְאָסָא עַד שֶׁמָּלַךְ אֲחַזְיָהוּ וְתִמְצָא אַרְבָּעִים וּשְׁתַּיִם שָׁנָה. וְעוֹד כָּתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר ״בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה... לְיוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב... מָלַךְ אֲחַזְיָהוּ בֶן יְהוֹרָם״ (מלכים ב ח:כה), וְכָתוּב אֶחָד אוֹמֵר ״וּבִשְׁנַת אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה... לְיוֹרָם... מָלַךְ אֲחַזְיָהוּ״ (מלכים ב ט:כט)! עַל כָּרְחֵינוּ, יְהוֹרָם אָבִיו לֹא מֵת אֶלָּא ״בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְיוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב״, שֶׁהֲרֵי כְּתִיב ״בִּשְׁנַת חָמֵשׁ לְיוֹרָם בֶּן אַחְאָב... מָלַךְ יְהוֹרָם בֶּן יְהוֹשָׁפָט... וּשְׁמֹנֶה שָׁנִים מָלָךְ״ (מלכים ב ח:טז-יז). וּמַה שֶּׁאָמַר שׁוּב ״בִּשְׁנַת אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה... לְיוֹרָם״ לְפִי שֶׁמָּלַךְ בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו שָׁנָה אַחַת, לְפִי שֶׁהָיָה אָבִיו נָגוּעַ בְּתַחֲלוּיִים קָשִׁים, כְּמוֹ שֶׁכָּתוּב ״וְאַחֲרֵי כָל זֹאת נְגָפוֹ ה׳ וְגוֹ׳״ (דברי הימים ב כא:יח), ״וַיַּמְלִיכוּ יֹשְׁבֵי יְרוּשָׁלַיִם אֶת אֲחַזְיָהוּ בְנוֹ הַקָּטָן״ (דברי הימים ב כב:א) שָׁנָה אַחַת בְּחַיֵּי אָבִיו; וְזֶהוּ דִּכְתִיב ״בִּשְׁנַת אַחַת עֶשְׂרֵה לְיוֹרָם״ (מלכים ב ט:כט); וּמַה שֶּׁאָמַר ״בִּשְׁנַת שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְיוֹרָם״ לְאַחַר שֶׁמֵּת אָבִיו, אָז הָיָה שְׁתֵּים עֶשְׂרֵה לְיוֹרָם:


**forty two years old** But in II Kings (8:26), Scripture says: “twenty-two years old.” How is this possible? Moreover, according to this, the son was two years his father’s senior, [because Joram ascended the throne at thirty-two and died eight years later, making him forty years old upon his death,] but two years before Joram his father was born, this decree was issued upon the house of David that it would end with the house of Ahab, and so Scripture states (below verse 7): “And from God was Ahaziah’s downfall, etc.” and on the day that Asa took Omri’s daughter for Jehoshaphat his son, this decree was issued. So it was taught in *Seder Olam* (ch. 17) and in the *Tosefta* of *Sotah* (12:3). But throughout Scripture we do not find that Jehoshaphat married Omri’s daughter, but I did find (above 18:1): “and he allied himself in marriage with Ahab,” and perhaps that was his sister that he married, and in the thirty first year of Asa he married her, as it is said in (I Kings 16:23): “And (sic) in the thirty-first year of Asa, etc. Omri reigned.” And when Asa married his daughter to Jehoshaphat his son, Omri gained importance, and they slew Tibni. Figure from the thirty-first year of Asa until Ahaziah reigned, and you will find forty two years. Further, one verse states (II Kings 8:25): “In the twelfth year of Joram the son of Ahab, etc., Ahaziah the son of Joram reigned,” and another verse states (ibid. 9:29): “And in the eleventh year of Joram son of Ahab, Ahaziah became king.” Perforce, his father did not die until the twelfth year of Joram son of Ahab, because it is written (ibid. 8: 16f.): “In the fifth year of Joram the son of Ahab, etc., Joram the son of Jehoshaphat became king, etc., and he reigned eight years.” And that which Scripture states further, “In the eleventh year of Joram,” is because he reigned one year during his father’s lifetime since his father was smitten with severe illnesses, as it is written (above 21:18.): “And after all this, the Lord plagued him, etc.” (22: 1) “And the inhabitants of Jerusalem made Ahaziah, his youngest son, king,” for one year during his father’s lifetime, and that is what is written: “In the eleventh year of Joram.” And where Scripture says: “In the twelfth year of Joram,” it means that after his father died, it was the twelfth year of Joram.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:5:1)


**וַיַּכּוּ הָרַמִּים.** כְּמוֹ הָאֲרַמִּים; הַרְבֵּה מְקוֹמוֹת חָסֵר אָלֶ״ף, כְּגוֹן ״לַעֲמָשָׂא תֹמְרוּ״ (שמואל ב יט:יד); וּפְעָמִים הָאָלֶ״ף נוֹסֶפֶת בַּתֵּיבָה, כְּגוֹן ״אֵיתָן הָאֶזְרָחִי״ (תהלים פט:א) – כְּמוֹ הַזְּרָחִי, וְאָלֶ״ף שֶׁל ״וְאַחֲוָתִי בְאָזְנֵיכֶם״ (איוב יג:יז):


**and the Arameans smote Joram** - Heb. הָרַמִּים, like הָאֲרַמִּים. In many places the “aleph” is missing, as for instance (II Sam. 19:14): “And to Amasa you shall say (תֹמְרוּ).” And sometimes the “aleph” is added to a word, for instance (Ps. 89:1): “Ethan the Ezrahite (הָאֶזְרָחִי),” like הַזַּרְחִי, [descendant of Zerah], and the “aleph” of (Job 13:17): “and to my speech (וְאַחְוָתִי) in your ears.”



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:6:1)


**כִּי הַמַּכִּים אֲשֶׁר הִכּוּהוּ.** פִּתְרוֹן: מִן הַמַּכִּים, וְכֵן בִּמְלָכִים (מלכים ב ח:כט):


**because of the smiters who smote him** כִּי הַמַּכִּים. The meaning is: מִן הַמַּכִּים, because of the smiters, and so it is in II Kings (8:29).


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:6:2)


**וַעֲזַרְיָהוּ בֶן יְהוֹרָם.** זֶה אֲחַזְיָהוּ, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר ״וַאֲחַזְיָהוּ מֶלֶךְ יְהוּדָה יָרַד לִרְאוֹת אֶת יוֹרָם״ (מלכים ב ט:טז):


**and Azariah the son of Jehoram** This is Ahaziah, and so Scripture states (ad loc.): “and Ahaziah the son of Joram went down to see Joram.”



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:7:1)


**תְּבוּסַת.** פִּתְרוֹן: עִרְבּוּב, כְּמוֹ ״מִתְבּוֹסֶסֶת בְּדָמָיִךְ״ (יחזקאל טז:ו):


**downfall** Heb. תְּבוּסַת. The meaning is confusion, like (Ezek. 16:6): “wallowing (מִתְבּוֹסֶסֶת) in your blood.”



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:8:1)


**וּבְנֵי אֲחֵי אֲחַזְיָהוּ.** וּמַה שֶׁלֹּא נֶאֱמַר וַאֲחֵי אֲחַזְיָהוּ, לְפִי שֶׁנֶּהֶרְגוּ בִּימֵי יְהוֹרָם אֲבִיהֶם, כְּדִכְתִיב לְעֵיל: ״וַיַּעֲלוּ בִיהוּדָה וַיִּבְקָעוּהָ... וְלֹא נִשְׁאַר... בֵּן כִּי אִם אֲחַזְיָהוּ קְטַן בָּנָיו״ (דברי הימים ב כא:יז):


**and the sons of Ahaziah’s brothers** He does not mention Ahaziah’s brothers because they were slain in the days of Joram their father, as it is written (above 21:17): “And they marched upon Judah and split it, etc., and no son was left to him except Jehoahaz, the youngest of his sons.”



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:10:1)


**וַתָּקָם וַתְּדַבֵּר.** לְשׁוֹן דֶּבֶר, וּבִמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יא:א) כְּתִיב ״וַתְּאַבֵּד״. וְעַל הַדּוֹר הַזֶּה אָמַר דָּוִד (תהלים יב:א) ״לַמְנַצֵּחַ עַל הַשְּׁמִינִית״ – שֶׁרָאָה בְּרוּחַ הַקֹּדֶשׁ שֶׁבַּדּוֹר שְׁמִינִי יֵהָרֵג כָּל זַרְעוֹ עַל יְדֵי עֲתַלְיָהוּ, שֶׁהֲרֵי מִשְּׁלֹמֹה עַד כָּאן שְׁמֹנָה דוֹרוֹת, וְהִתְפַּלֵּל שֶׁיִּשְׁתַּיֵּר זַרְעוֹ לְזִכָּרוֹן, וְאוֹמֵר (תהלים יב:ב): ״הוֹשִׁיעָה ה׳ כִּי גָמַר חָסִיד וְגוֹמֵר״. וּבִמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יא:ב) אוֹמֵר שֶׁעַל יְדֵי סַם הַמָּוֶת הֲרָגָתַם, שֶׁהֲרֵי כְּתִיב שָׁם ״וַתִּגְנֹב אוֹתוֹ מִתּוֹךְ בְּנֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ הַמְּמוּתִים״, וְקָרִינָן ״הַמּוּמָתִים״, וְיֵשׁ אֵם לַמִּקְרָא וְיֵשׁ אֵם לַמְּסוֹרֶת (סוכה ו:): ״הַמּוּמָתִים״ – מַשְׁמַע שֶׁכְּבָר מֵתוּ; וְאֵם לַמְּסוֹרֶת ״הַמְּמוּתִים״ – שֶׁמֵּתִים וְהוֹלְכִים עַל יְדֵי יִסּוּרִים, לְשׁוֹן הֹוֶה וְלֹא לְאַלְתַּר. וְזֶהוּ מִיתַת כִּשּׁוּף וְסַם הַמָּוֶת, שֶׁלֹּא מֵתִים לְאַלְתַּר, הוֹלְכִים וּמְדֻכִּים בְּתַחֲלוּאִים, וְשׁוּב מֵתִים; וְדֻגְמָא: ״מְמוֹתֵי תַחֲלוּאֵי רָעָב״ (ירמיה טז:ד; ירמיה יד:יח), כִּי גַּם הֵם אֵינָם מֵתִים לְאַלְתַּר:


**she arose and destroyed** - Heb. וַתְּדַבֵּר, an expression of דֶּבֶר, pestilence. In II Kings (11:1), it is written: וַתְּאַבֵּד. Concerning this generation, David recited: (Ps. 12:1): “For the conductor on the sheminith,” for David foresaw with the holy spirit that in the eighth generation [of his dynasty] all his descendants would be slain by Athaliah, for there are eight generations from Solomon till here. And he prayed that his seed would survive for a remembrance, and said, (ibid. verse 2): “Save, O Lord, for the pious are gone, etc.” In II Kings (11:2), Scripture states that she killed them by poisoning them, for it is written: “and she stole him away from the dying (הַמְמוֹתִים) children of the king”; we read: הַמּוּמָתִים the slain. Both the traditional reading and the masoretic text have authority. הַמּוּמָתִים means that they already died, and the masoretic text in which the word used is הַמְמוֹתִים means that they were dying in agony. This is written in the continuous present tense, i.e., not all at once but continuously being crushed by illnesses, and then dying. An example is (Jer. 16:4): “Dying of (מְמוֹתֵי) sicknesses they shall die, etc. and with famine,” because they too will not die immediately.



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 22:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/22:11:1)


**בַּחֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת.** תַּרְגּוּם: ״בְּאִדְרוֹן בֵּית עַרְסָתָא״, כְּלוֹמַר: בָּעֲזָרָה, בְּחֶדֶר שְׁכִיבַת הַכֹּהֲנִים. וְרַבֵּינוּ שְׁלֹמֹה זַצַ״ל פֵּירֵשׁ: בַּעֲלִיַּת בֵּית קֹדֶשׁ הַקֳּדָשִׁים, כְּדִכְתִיב בִּמְלָכִים ״וַיְהִי אִתָּהּ״ בְּ״בֵית ה׳ מִתְחַבֵּא וְגוֹ׳״ (מלכים ב י:ג); וְעָלָיו אָמַר דָּוִד: ״כִּי יִצְפְּנֵנִי בְּסֻכֹּה וְגוֹ׳״ (תהלים כז:ה). וְקוֹרֵהוּ ״חֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת״ בִּמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יא:ב), עַל שֵׁם ״בֵּין שָׁדַי יָלִין״ (שיר השירים א:יג); וְהוּא שֶׁיִּסֵּד הַקַּלִּיר (קרא ר׳ אליעזר ובזכרונות דיום ראשון דראש השנה יסד זה עיין שם): ״אִם פָּץ לְמַטּוֹת, נָפַץ חֲדַר הַמִּטּוֹת, לְכַפּוֹר יִזְכּוֹר חֻרְבַּן הַבַּיִת יְכַפֵּר עֲלֵיכֶם״:


**in the bed chamber** The *Targum* [*Jonathan* to II Kings 11:2] renders: in the chamber of the beds, i.e., in the forecourt, in the room where the priests sleep, but Rabbi Solomon of blessed memory (Rashi ad loc) explained it as the attic of the Holy of Holies, as it is written in Kings (verse 3): “And he hid with her in the House of the Lord, etc.,” and concerning him, David said, (Ps. 27:5): “For He will hide me in His Tabernacle, etc.” He calls it the bed chamber based on (Song 1:13): “between my breasts he will lie,” and that is what the Kallir, Rabbi Eliezer Kara composed (in Zichronoth for the first day of Rosh Hashanah in the section devoted to the letter “nun”): If He said to cause them to totter, the smashing of the bed-chamber He will remember for an atonement. The destruction of the Temple will atone for them.



## Chapter 23


### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:3:1)


**וַיִּכְרֹת כָּל הַקָּהָל בְּרִית בְּבֵית הָאֱלֹהִים עִם הַמֶּלֶךְ.** שֶׁיִּהְיוּ לְעֶזְרָתוֹ:


**And the entire congregation enacted a covenant in the House of God with the king** that they would support him.



### Verse 4

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:4:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:4:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:4:1)


**בָּאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת.** וְגַם יוֹצְאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת, כְּדִכְתִיב בְּסָמוּךְ **וַיִּקְחוּ אִישׁ אֶת אֲנָשָׁיו בָּאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת עִם יוֹצְאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת**. הַמִּשְׁמָרוֹת הָיוּ מִתְחַדְּשׁוֹת בְּשַׁבָּת (תענית ב:ו-ז): כְּהוּנָּה וּלְוִיָּה אַחַת יוֹצְאָה וְאַחַת נִכְנָסָה; וְאָז עִכֵּב יְהוֹיָדָע שָׁם מִשְׁמָר הַיּוֹצֵא עִם מִשְׁמָר הַנִּכְנָס, וְחִלְּקָן לִשְׁלֹשָׁה חֲלָקִים:


**of those coming in on the Sabbath** And also those leaving on the Sabbath, as is written below (verse 8): “and each one took his men, those coming in on the Sabbath with those leaving on the Sabbath.” The watches were renewed on the Sabbath (See *Taanith* 2:6,7), viz. the priests and the Levites; one [watch] would leave and one would come in. Jehoiada detained the watch that was leaving with the watch that was coming in, and divided them into three divisions.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:1)


**וְהַשְּׁלִישִׁית בְּבֵית הַמֶּלֶךְ.** לִשְׁמֹר שֶׁלֹּא יִקָּבְצוּ עָלָיו לַהֲרֹג אוֹתוֹ:


**And a third shall be in the king’s palace** to guard that they would not gather about him to assassinate him.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:2)


**וְהַשְּׁלִישִׁית.** הִיא הַשֵּׁנִית:


**and a third** This is the second one.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:5:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:5:3)


**בְּשַׁעַר הַיְסוֹד.** וּבִמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יא:ו) כְּתִיב ״וּבְשַׁעַר סוּר״, וּבְיוֹמָא יְרוּשַׁלְמִי מְפֹרָשׁ (ירושלמי עירובין ה:א): שִׁבְעָה שֵׁמוֹת הָיוּ לוֹ לְשַׁעַר הַיְסוֹד; ״סוּר״ – עַל שֵׁם שֶׁהָיוּ מְטַהֲרִים שָׁם אֶת הַמְצֹרָעִים עַל בֹּהֶן יָדָם, וְעַל שֵׁם ״סוּרוּ טָמֵא קָרְאוּ לָמוֹ״ (איכה ד:טו):


**in the Yesod Gate** But in II Kings (ad loc.) it is written “the Sur Gate.” In *Yoma Yerushalmi* (in extant editions it is found in *Erubin Yerushalmi* 5:1) it is explained that the Yesod Gate had seven names: 1) the Sur Gate, because they would purify those smitten with zaraath there [by placing the blood of the guilt offering and the oil] on their thumbs, reminiscent of [the verse] (Lam. 4:15): “They called out to them: ‘Depart (סוּרוּ), unclean [ones]!’”



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:6:1)


**וְכָל הָעָם יִשְׁמְרוּ.** שֶׁלֹּא יָבוֹאוּ **בֵית ה׳**:


**and all the people shall keep** lest they enter the House of the Lord.



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:7:1)


**וְכֵלָיו בְּיָדוֹ.** כְּלֵי מִלְחַמְתּוֹ בְּיָדוֹ:


**with his weapons in his hand** His weapons in his hand.



### Verse 8

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:8:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:8:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:8:1)


**בָּאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת עִם יוֹצְאֵי הַשַּׁבָּת כִּי לֹא פָטַר.** אוֹתָן **מַחְלְקוֹת** הַיּוֹצְאִים לָלֶכֶת לְבֵיתָם, אֶלָּא עִכְּבָם שָׁם עִם הַבָּאִים; דֻּגְמָא: ״בַּלְּשָׁכוֹת פְּטוּרִים״ (דברי הימים א ט:לג) – כְּלוֹמַר: פְּטוּרִים הָיוּ מִכָּל מְלָאכָה שֶׁבָּעֲזָרָה, כִּי אִם לִשְׁמֹר הַלְּשָׁכוֹת, כִּי יוֹמָם וָלַיְלָה חוֹבָה עֲלֵיהֶם בַּמְּלָאכָה לִשְׁמֹר הָעֲזָרוֹת וְהַלְּשָׁכוֹת, לְפִיכָךְ הָיוּ פְּטוּרִים מִכָּל שְׁאָר מְלֶאכֶת הָעֲזָרָה:


**of those coming in on the Sabbath with those leaving on the Sabbath, for he did not dismiss** those divisions who were leaving to go home, but he detained them with those who were arriving. A similarity to [the word פָּטַר] is found in (I Chron. 9:33): “in the chambers, who are exempt (פְּטוּרִים)”; i.e., they were exempt from all work in the forecourt, for day and night it was incumbent upon them to perform the work to guard the forecourts and the chambers; they were therefore exempt from all other work in the forecourt.



### Verse 10

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:10:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:10:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:10:1)


**וְאִישׁ שִׁלְחוֹ בְיָדוֹ.** כְּלִי זֵינוֹ, כְּמוֹ ״וּבְעַד הַשֶּׁלַח״ דְּיוֹאֵל (יואל ב:ח):


**and each one with his sword in his hand** - Heb. שִׁלְחוֹ his weapon, like (Joel 2:8): “and upon the sword (הַשֶּׁלַח).”



### Verse 11

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:11:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:11:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:11:1)


**אֶת הַנֵּזֶר.** הִיא הָעֲטָרָה שֶׁלָּקַח דָּוִד מֵרַבַּת בְּנֵי עַמּוֹן, כְּדִכְתִיב ״וַיִּקַּח דָּוִד עֲטֶרֶת מַלְכָּם וַתְּהִי עַל רֹאשׁ דָּוִד״ (דברי הימים א כ:ב). וּמִשָּׁם וָהָלְאָה הָיוּ מַלְכֵי יְהוּדָה מְנִיחִים אוֹתָהּ הַנֵּזֶר עַל רֹאשָׁם:


**the crown** This is the crown that David took from Rabbah of the children of Ammon, as it is written (I Chron 20:2): “And David took the crown of Malkam off his head, etc., and it was on David’s head.” From then on, the kings of Judah would wear that crown on their heads.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:11:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:11:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:11:2)


**וְאֶת הָעֵדוּת.** זֶה סֵפֶר תּוֹרָה, וְכֵן הוּא אוֹמֵר ״וְקָרָא בוֹ כָּל יְמֵי חַיָּיו״ (דברים יז:יט). וְרַבּוֹתֵינוּ אָמְרוּ (עבודה זרה מד.): **הַנֵּזֶר** זֶה **הָעֵדוּת**, שֶׁכָּל הָרָאוּי לַמַּלְכוּת הוֹלַמְתּוֹ, וְשֶׁאֵינוֹ רָאוּי לַמַּלְכוּת אֵינָהּ הוֹלַמְתּוֹ:


**and the testimony** This is a Sepher Torah, and so Scripture states (Deut. 17:19): “and he shall read from it as long as he lives.” Our Sages stated (*Abodah Zarah* 4a), however, that the crown itself was the testimony, for it would not fit anyone unfit for the throne.



### Verse 13

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:13:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:13:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:13:1)


**עוֹמֵד עַל עַמּוּדוֹ.** כְּדַאֲמַר ״וַיַּעֲמֹד הַמֶּלֶךְ עַל הָעַמּוּד״ (מלכים ב כג:ג), ״כִּי עָשָׂה שְׁלֹמֹה כִּיּוֹר נְחֹשֶׁת וְגוֹ׳ וַיַּעֲמֹד עָלָיו וַיְבָרֵךְ עַל בִּרְכָּיו״ (דברי הימים ב ו:יג). וְעַד הַיּוֹם מִנְהָג כְּשֶׁמְּוַעֲדִים יַחַד, עוֹמֵד הַמֶּלֶךְ אוֹ הַדּוּכוֹס בְּמָקוֹם גָּבוֹהַּ כְּדֵי שֶׁיּוּכְלוּ לִרְאוֹתוֹ, לִשְׁמֹעַ אֶת דְּבָרוֹ:


**standing on his stand** as it says (above 6:12f.): “And he stood before the altar of the Lord, etc. for Solomon had made a copper laver, etc., and he stood on it and kneeled on his knees.” It is still customary today that when they assemble together, the king or the duke stands on a high place, so that they should be able to see him and hear his words.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:13:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:13:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:13:2)


**קֶשֶׁר קֶשֶׁר.** מֶרֶד, מֶרֶד:


**“Treason! Treason!”** Rebellion! Rebellion!



### Verse 14

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:14:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:1)


**הַשְּׂדֵרוֹת.** פֵּירוּשׁ: מְקוֹם מְבוֹא הַמֶּלֶךְ מִבֵּיתוֹ לְבֵית ה׳. לֹא יָדַעְתִּי מַה לָּשׁוֹן שְׂדֵרוֹת, וְיוֹנָתָן תִּרְגֵּם (תרגום יונתן מלכים ב יא:ח): ״סִידְרַיָּא״:


**the ranks** - Heb. הַשְּׂדֵרוֹת. Rashi (II Kings 11:8) explains: the place of the king’s entry from his palace to the House of the Lord. I do not know the origin of the word, and *Jonathan* rendered it as “rows” [like סְדָרִים].


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:14:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:2)


**וְהַבָּא אַחֲרֶיהָ יוּמַת.** אַל תַּנִּיחוּהָ לָצֵאת דֶּרֶךְ מוֹצָא הָעֲזָרָה אֶל הָעִיר, פֶּן תִּמְצָא עוֹזְרִים מִתְחַבְּרִים אֵלֶיהָ:


**and whoever follows her is to be put to death** Do not allow her to go out by way of the exit of the forecourt to the city, lest she find helpers who will join her.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:14:3

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:3](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:3)


**וְהַבָּא אַחֲרֶיהָ.** לָעֶזְרָה:


**and whoever follows her** to her aid.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:14:4

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:4](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:14:4)


**יוּמַת.** הֲמִיתוּהוּ:


**is to be put to death** Put him to death.



### Verse 15

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:15:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:15:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:15:1)


**וַיָּשִׂימוּ לָהּ יָדַיִם.** מָקוֹם לָנוּס:


**And they made a place for her** a place to escape.



### Verse 16

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:16:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:16:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:16:1)


**בְּרִית בֵּינוֹ וּבֵין כָּל הָעָם וּבֵין הַמֶּלֶךְ.** שֶׁיְּהוּ כֻלָּם הַמֶּלֶךְ וְהָעָם אַחֲרֵי ה׳:


**between himself and all the people and the king** that all of them, the king and the people, should follow the Lord.



### Verse 18

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:18:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:18:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:18:1)


**פְּקֻדּוֹת.** מְמֻנִּים עַל זֶה, לְהַעֲלוֹת עוֹלוֹת וְכָל הָאָמוּר בַּפָּרָשָׁה; דּוּגְמָא: ״וַיִּשְׁמַע פַּשְׁחוּר וְגוֹ׳ וְהוּא פָּקִיד נָגִיד בֵּית ה׳״ (ירמיהו כ:א):


**appointees** - Heb. פְּקֻדּוֹת, people appointed to offer burnt offerings and all that is mentioned in this chapter. An example is (Jer. 20:1): “And Pashhur heard, etc. and he was the chief officer (פָּקִיד) in the House of the Lord.”



### Verse 19

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 23:19:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:19:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/23:19:1)


**וְלֹא יָבֹא טָמֵא לְכָל דָּבָר.** אַף הָיוּ פְּקִידִים שֶׁלֹּא יַנִּיחוּ לִיכָּנֵס בָּעֲזָרָה שׁוּם טָמֵא מִשּׁוּם צֹרֶךְ כָּל דָּבָר:


**so that no one who was unclean should enter for any matter** There were also appointees [whose job it was] not to allow any unclean person to enter the forecourt for any reason.



## Chapter 24


### Verse 2

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:2:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:2:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:2:1)


**כָּל יְמֵי יְהוֹיָדָע.** וּבִמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יב:ג) כְּתִיב ״כָּל יָמָיו אֲשֶׁר הוֹרָהוּ הַכֹּהֵן״, אֲבָל לְאַחַר שֶׁמֵּת, אָז בָּאוּ שָׂרֵי הַמֶּלֶךְ לְהִשְׁתַּחֲווֹת לוֹ לַעֲשׂוֹתוֹ קֹדֶשׁ. וּבַמִּדְרָשׁ מְפֹרָשׁ (תנחומא, וארא טז): לְפִי שֶׁנִּטְמַן בְּבֵית קֹדֶשׁ הַקֳּדָשִׁים וְלֹא הוּזַק, אָמְרוּ: כְּדַי אַתָּה לִהְיוֹת אֱלוֹהַּ:


**all the days of Jehoiada** And in II Kings (12:3) it is written: “all his days, [he did] that which Jehoiada the priest instructed him,” but after he died, the officers of the king came to prostrate themselves to him and to deify him (below verse 17). In the midrash (*Exod. Rabbah* 8:2, *Tan.* Buber, *Va’era*) it is explained that, because he hid in the Holy of Holies and was not harmed, they said, “You are fit to be a god.”



### Verse 3

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:3:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:3:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:3:1)


**וַיּוֹלֶד בָּנִים וּבָנוֹת.** לְפִי שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר לְמַטָּה (לקמן, כב) שֶׁהָרַג יוֹאָשׁ אֶת בְּנֵי יְהוֹיָדָע, הֻצְרַךְ לוֹמַר שֶׁהָיוּ לוֹ בָּנִים:


**and he begot sons and daughters** Because it says below (verse 25) that Joash slew the sons of Jehoiada, it was necessary to note that he had sons.



### Verse 5

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:5:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:5:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:5:1)


**וְקִבְצוּ מִכָּל יִשְׂרָאֵל.** כְּלוֹמַר: קִבְצוּ אוֹתוֹ **כֶּסֶף** שֶׁהָיוּ יִשְׂרָאֵל נוֹתְנִים **מִדֵּי שָׁנָה בְּשָׁנָה**, כְּדִכְתִיב ״כָּל הָעוֹבֵר עַל הַפְּקוּדִים״ (שמות ל:יג) וּבִמְלָכִים ״כָּל כֶּסֶף הַקֳּדָשִׁים אֲשֶׁר יוּבָא בֵּית ה׳ כֶּסֶף עוֹבֵר וְגוֹ׳״ (מלכים ב יב:ה); שֶׁשָּׁלֹשׁ כְּסָפִים הָיוּ: ״כֶּסֶף עוֹבֵר״ שְׁקָלִים בְּכָל שָׁנָה, שֶׁנֶּאֱמַר ״כָּל הָעוֹבֵר עַל הַפְּקוּדִים״ (שמות ל:יג) – ״עוֹבֵר״ עַל שֵׁם הָעוֹבֵר; ״כֶּסֶף נַפְשׁוֹת עֶרְכּוֹ״ (מלכים ב יב:ה) – עַל אִישׁ שֶׁהִתְנַדֵּב עֵרֶךְ נַפְשׁוֹ, כְּמוֹ הָאוֹמֵר עֶרְכִּי עָלַי; וְכָל כֶּסֶף אֲשֶׁר יַעֲלֶה עַל לֵב אִישׁ נְדָבָה לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת:


**and gather money from all Israel** i.e., Gather the money that the Israelites were accustomed to giving yearly, as it is written (Exod. 30:14): “Each one included in the census,” and in II Kings (12: 5): “All money of the hallowed things that is brought to the House of the Lord, in current money, etc.” There were three types of money: (1) the money of all those counted in the census, viz. the shekels for every year, as it is said (Exod. 30:14): “Each one included in the census,” lit., “[the money of anyone] who passes,” reminiscent of “each one who passes.” (2) Personal donations - money donated by individuals to reflect the value of their souls, like one who says, “My value shall be incumbent upon me to donate.” (3) All donations inspired by a person’s heart for the repairs of the Temple.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:5:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:5:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:5:2)


**וְאַתֶּם תְּמַהֲרוּ לַדָּבָר וְלֹא מִהֲרוּ הַלְוִיִּם.** מְפֹרָשׁ בְּטוֹב בִּמְלָכִים (מלכים ב יב:ו) שֶׁאָמַר הַמֶּלֶךְ ״יִקְחוּ לָהֶם הַכֹּהֲנִים אִישׁ מֵאֵת מַכָּרוֹ״, וְיִתְעַצְּלוּ בַּדָּבָר לְהַטְרִיחַ מַכָּרֵיהֶם, וּכְשֶׁרָאָה הַמֶּלֶךְ כֵּן אָמַר לָהֶם (מלכים ב יב:ח) ״וְעַתָּה אַל תִּקְחוּ כֶּסֶף מֵאֵת מַכָּרֵיכֶם כִּי לְבֶדֶק הַבַּיִת תִּתְּנוּהוּ״, זֶהוּ שֶׁאָמַר כָּאן וְאַתֶּם תְּמַהֲרוּ לַדָּבָר וְלֹא מִהֲרוּ הַלְוִיִּם:


**and hasten to the matter,” but the Levites did not hasten** It is explained in II Kings (12:6f.) that the king said, “Let the priests take [the money] for themselves, each one from his acquaintance,” but they neglected to impose upon their acquaintances, and when the king saw this, he said to them, (ibid. verse 8): “Do not take money from your acquaintances [any longer], but deliver it for the repairs of the House.” This is what is written here: “’and hasten to the matter,’ but the Levites did not hasten.”



### Verse 6

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:6:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:6:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:6:1)


**מַשְׂאַת מֹשֶׁה עֶבֶד ה׳.** כְּלוֹמַר: שְׁקָלִים שֶׁצִּוָּה עֲלֵיהֶם בַּמִּדְבָּר (שמות ל:יג-טז):


**the tax of Moses the servant of the Lord** i.e., the shekels which he mandated upon them in the desert.


###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:6:2

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:6:2](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:6:2)


**מַשְׂאַת.** עַל שֵׁם ״כִּי תִשָּׂא״ (שמות ל:יב):


**the tax of** - Heb. מַשְׂאַת, reminiscent of (Exod. 30:12): “When you take (כִּי תִשָּׂא) [the sum].”



### Verse 7

###### Rashi on II Chronicles 24:7:1

[https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:7:1](https://torahapp.org/share/book/Rashi%20on%20II%20Chronicles/r/24:7:1)
